### The
# Secret of All Secrets
## from the <term>Macrocosm and Microcosm</term>,
<note>original: "ex Macrocosmo et Microcosmo"; these terms refer to the ancient philosophical idea that the "Great World" (the universe) is reflected in the "Small World" (the human being).</note>
### or
## the Golden Concept
### of the
## Most Hidden Secrets
### of the
# <term>Rose and Gold Cross</term>
<note>This refers to the Rosicrucians, a legendary spiritual and cultural movement that arose in Europe in the early 17th century, claiming to possess secret wisdom about nature and the divine.</note>
### with
## Their Three Stones of Wonders.
<note>In the context of alchemy, "stones" often refer to the results of the "Great Work," such as the Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to grant eternal life or transform base metals into gold.</note>

## Leipzig, 1788.
## Published by Adam Friedrich Böhme.

### How to make the true Alkahest from the Sun <note>original: ☉ (Sol), the alchemical symbol for gold</note>.

**I**t is done as follows: take 10 pounds <note>original: ℔ (libra)</note> of fully mature gold, fill two large retorts <term>⚗️</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</note> with it, and place them in a furnace with large receivers, everything well-luted <note>Sealed with "lutum," a clay or paste used to make joints airtight</note>. Once the luting is quite dry, drive everything over that will go, by degrees <note>original: ꝑ gradus</note>, over the course of twice forty-eight hours. Then let the fire <note>original: △ (Ignis)</note> go out. When it has cooled a little, carefully remove the receiver and quickly plug it. Pour your distilled chaos <note>A term for the crude, unrefined matter resulting from the first distillation</note> into a new and clean flask, attach a still-head <note>A "helm" or alembic cover</note> and a receiver, everything well-luted, and distill everything volatile by degrees in a sand-bath. Thus, along with the phlegm <note>The watery, inactive component of a distillate</note>, a spirit will come over, like a spirit of wine <note>original: Spiritus Vini (alcohol)</note>. Rectify <note>Purify a liquid by repeated distillation</note> this from its phlegm in a water bath <note>original: B. M. (Balneum Mariae)</note> seven times, so that all the phlegm is removed from it.

Keep this spirit of wine <note>original: Sp. V.</note> well. Put the aforementioned <margin>⸫</margin> corrosive substance into a retort <term>⚗️</term> and place it

Put it with skill and art <note>original: m. e. a. (modis et artis)</note> into a <term>sublimation vessel</term> and <term>sublime</term> it; in the beginning, the <term>sublimate</term> <note>The solid substance produced by heating a material until it turns to vapor and then collects as a solid on a cooler surface</note> will not be particularly colored. Take that <term>sublimate</term> and grind it again with the residue at the bottom and <term>sublime</term> it once more. Do this a third time as well, and then your <term>sublimate</term> will become like an egg yolk. <term>Dissolve</term> <note>original: solvire</note> this in distilled rainwater <term>water</term> <note>alchemical symbol for water: ▽</note>, and <term>precipitate</term> it a little with a distilled vinegar. Pour off the clear liquid and add more <term>water</term> <note>▽</note> to it, and do this as often as necessary until no more <term>water</term> <note>▽</note> tastes of <term>salt</term> <note>alchemical symbol for salt: 🜔</note>. If the poured-off <term>water</term> <note>▽</note> is distilled away, one gets the <term>salt</term> <note>🜔</note> back again. 

When the <term>precipitate</term> <note>The solid that settles out of a liquid solution</note> is quite dry, put it into a <term>retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-curving neck: ⚗</note> and pour all the aforementioned <term>liquid</term> upon it. Seal the vessel <note>original: schmelz solche zu; literally "melt it shut"</note> and set it there to <term>digest</term> <note>To heat steadily and gently</note> for 14 <term>days</term> <note>original: ♎ (Libra), here used as a symbol for a day-night cycle</note>. Then distill it over in a <term>dry bath</term> <note>original: B. sicco (Balneum siccum); heating via sand or ash</note> after everything has been previously <term>luted</term> <note>Sealed with a clay-like paste to prevent the escape of vapors</note>. <term>Cohobate</term> <note>The process of repeatedly pouring a distilled liquid back onto its residue and re-distilling it</note> until all the <term>soul</term> <note>original: anima; the essential or "spiritual" part of the substance</note> comes over, leaving behind only a useless <term>water</term> <note>▽</note>. 

Then take 6 <term>Loth</term> <note>A historical unit of weight, roughly 1/2 ounce or 15 grams</note> of purified <term>mercury</term> <note>alchemical symbol: ☿</note> and pour 18 <term>Loth</term> of the <term>liquid</term> upon it in a <term>retort</term> <note>⚗</note>. Everything must be well <term>luted</term> and previously <term>digested</term> for 6 <term>days</term> <note>♎</note>. Then, with a receiver-vessel attached with lute, distill it over with skill and art. If it should not all rise over at once, repeat your <term>cohobation</term> again so that nothing remains behind except for a black earth. Then pour this <term>liquid</term> again onto 6 <term>Loth</term> of <term>mercury</term> <note>☿</note> and proceed in all things as has been noted. Repeat this 7 times, and thus you will obtain a noble <term>Alkahest</term> <note>The fabled universal solvent capable of dissolving every substance</note> from the **Mineral Kingdom.**

Although this **Alkahest** <note>The legendary "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, capable of dissolving any substance into its primary components</note> is difficult to make, it is nevertheless the most important of all. Pour 8 **Loth** <note>A historical unit of weight used in German-speaking lands, roughly equivalent to 14 to 17 grams</note> of this [liquor] onto one **Loth** of prepared **Gold** <note>alchemical symbol: ☉</note> and **Antimony** <note>alchemical symbol: ♆; here likely referring to the purified metal or "regulus"</note> into a <image-desc>alchemical symbol for a retort</image-desc> and **digest** <note>To apply gentle, consistent heat over a long period</note> it with skill and art <note>original: m. e. a. (modis et artis)</note> for 4 weeks. Then, distil your noble **Liquor** over, put it into a vial and seal it carefully, and let it pass through the colors; in the end, it will **coagulate** <note>Change from a liquid to a solid state</note> into a red **carbuncle** <note>A term for a deep red gemstone, often used to describe the completed Philosopher's Stone</note>. With the "double Alkahest," the work can be augmented **to infinity** <note>original: in infinitum</note>, for the more often this is done, the more penetrating it becomes, and you will obtain a treasure that neither Emperor nor King can pay for.

## To make the Alkahest from the Vegetable Kingdom.

Take a good, strong wine, set it aside, and let it **digest** for a few days with a little fermented dough or yeast. Distil from this a **Spirit of Wine** <note>original: Sp. Vini; concentrated alcohol or ethanol</note>; distil this 7 times in very gentle heat so that it is separated from all **phlegm** <note>The watery, tasteless, and energetically "dead" part of a distilled substance</note>. Keep this safely in a large glass. Meanwhile, keep the remaining liquid in gentle **putrefaction** <note>A process of slow fermentation or rotting used to break down the "body" of a substance</note> so that it becomes quite sour. Then, distil off all the **phlegm** from it until only a thick juice remains, along with the **vinegar** <note>image-desc: alchemical symbol for vinegar (Acetum)</note> at the same time. **Rectify** <note>To purify a substance through repeated distillation</note> the vinegar in a <image-desc>alchemical symbol for a retort</image-desc> so that all the **phlegm** is removed from it. Save the **vinegar** <note>image-desc: alchemical symbol for vinegar (Acetum)</note>; also take the **phlegm** and pour it onto the thick liquor and

Extract all the **Antimony** <note>alchemical symbol: ♁; here likely referring to a prepared form of the mineral</note>, pour the extractions clearly together into a tall flask, and distill in a **water bath** <note>original: B. M. (Balneum Mariae); a vessel of hot water used to provide gentle, even heat</note> all the **phlegm** <note>The watery, inactive portion of a distilled substance</note> until a red **sulfur** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜍; in alchemy, "sulfur" represents the principle of combustibility and soul</note> remains. Extract this once more with the phlegm so that all the **dregs** <note>original: feces</note> are removed; pour all the extractions together onto the fresh material and distill off all the phlegm again until a red **sulfur** remains. 

This you can now extract once more using only as much **rectified spirit of wine** <note>original: Sp. V. R. (Spiritus Vini Rectificatus); highly concentrated alcohol</note> as needed from the above, and store it well in a vial as a juice, ensuring that one does not lose much of the spirit. Then take all the **gold** <note>alchemical symbol: ☉</note>, **calcine** <note>To heat a substance to high temperatures in order to oxidize it or reduce it to a powder</note> it a little, grind it fine, and extract all the **gold** with the phlegm. Pour the extractions together into a flask and distill all the phlegm until a **salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔; the solid, stabilizing principle in alchemy</note> remains. Put all the phlegm together with the above into glasses, and set it once more into **putrefaction** <note>A process of warm digestion or fermentation meant to break down substances</note> for one month. 

Take your **salt**, and put it into a thick, clean **crucible** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜊</note>, ensuring that no coals fall into it, until it becomes blue-green. Then take it out, grind it fine, put it into a large vial, and pour **sal-ammoniac** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜹; ammonium chloride, often used as a flux or to help substances rise in distillation</note> and spirit onto it. Let it **putrefy** together, well-sealed, for 4 weeks. Then pour it into a flask, ensuring everything is well **luted** <note>Sealed with airtight clay or cement to prevent the escape of vapors</note>, or better yet, into a large, long-necked **retort** <note>alchemical symbol: 🝪; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note>, and distill everything over. If the **salt** has not all risen over, take the **salt** which remains in the **retort**, where one must break the vessel [to retrieve it]; however, if one wishes to be **sparing** <note>original: menagiren; to economize or manage carefully</note> with them, one takes a flask from which—

...by which the **salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note> is much easier to bring forth, so that one does not need to break the flask. M.</note>, and distill them over **in the proper manner** <note>m. e. a.</note>. Thus you obtain the true **Star of Gold** <note>original: Astrum ☉is (Astrum Solis); the quintessence or celestial virtue of gold</note>, as the right **soul of wine** <note>original: animam Vini</note>. ### The Alkahest from animal and vegetable sources together.

Take a good glossy soot <note>A shiny, resinous soot often collected from chimneys where wood was burned; used by alchemists as a source of ammonia</note>, crush it small and dissolve it in fresh <term>urine</term> <note>alchemical symbol: □</note> taken from a healthy person who drinks wine; filter the solution through, for the <term>urine</term> <note>alchemical symbol: □</note> takes no more from the soot than is necessary for it; place this into stone vessels and cover them with paper, and set the vessels aside for <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The process of allowing a substance to rot or ferment through gentle warmth, which alchemists believed "unlocked" the inner virtues of the material</note> for 3 months, or even 6 months, for the longer it putrefies, the better it is.

Note, you must have at least 20 cans of <term>urine</term> <note>alchemical symbol: □</note> for every 8 <term>pounds</term> <note>alchemical symbol: ℔</note> of soot. When your <term>putrefaction</term> is over, take a large flask and place a large helm <note>The cap of a distilling apparatus, also called an alembic head, where vapors collect and condense</note> upon it; pour enough into it, lute <note>To seal the joints of the glass apparatus with a "lute" or paste to prevent precious vapors from escaping</note> everything well, and distill all the volatile spirit over with a gentle <term>fire</term> <note>alchemical symbol: Δ</note>. Pour the residue out of the flask into another vessel, and put fresh material back into the flask; distill the volatile spirit from it again and proceed likewise with all the rest. Rectify <note>To purify a liquid through repeated distillation, often seven times in alchemy to reach a state of perfection</note> the spirit 7 times, so that everything

[flask and place it so that the] phlegm <note>In alchemy, "phlegm" refers to the watery, tasteless, and inactive liquid that comes over first during distillation</note> comes off it, and the spirit becomes quite pure; keep this in a tall flask. Pour the liquid back into the flask and distill all the phlegm from it until the heavy spirits rise. Then quickly attach another receiver <note>A vessel designed to catch and condense the vapors from the still</note>, and a vehement spirit and **Spirit of Wine** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔; here referring to a concentrated volatile alcohol or essence</note> will come over; a great deal of volatile **Spirit of Wine** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note> will also attach itself [to the glass]. When nothing more will come over, finally give it strong **Fire** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜂</note>, so that everything in the bottom of the flask glows strongly. Then take everything that has risen and come over into another flask, including the volatile **Spirit of Wine** from the alembic head <note>The "helm" or cap of the still</note>, and distill it several more times in a low flask in a sand bath <note>A container filled with sand used to provide even, indirect heat to a glass vessel</note>, and separate all the phlegm from it. Then pour the aforementioned spirit into it as well, and distill them both together with proper skill <note>original: m. e. a. (modo et arte), a common phrase meaning "according to the rules of the art"</note>. Keep these well along with the **Spirit of Wine**, for one must not separate them. 

From the **Salt** <note>original symbol: 🜔; though the scribe uses the same symbol for 'Spirit of Wine' above, the context of grinding and melting indicates he now refers to the 'Salt' or solid residue</note> make the **Salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note> entirely: take it out of the flask, rub it small and set it to the **Fire** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜂</note> so that it may be ignited by it. Then pour as much of the phlegm upon it and extract all the **Salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note>. Then distill all the phlegm off in a flask down to the **Salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note>. Keep all the phlegm well; put it into **putrefaction** <note>A slow process of digestion or fermentation in gentle heat</note>, for the **volatile earth** <note>Latin: Terra volatilis</note> is still hidden within it. Put the **Salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note> into a good strong crucible and melt it until it becomes green and blue. Take it out and rub it small. Put your **Salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note> into a **pelican** <note>A specialized circulatory vessel with two side-arms that feed vapors back into the main body, named for its resemblance to a bird pecking its own chest</note> and pour all the double **Spirit of Vitriol** <note>alchemical symbol: Sp. 🜚ii; a highly concentrated sulfuric acid</note> upon it.

(modo et arte)</note>, to putrefy for 2 months in gentle warmth; then pour everything together into a flask, and place an **alembic head** <note>the 'helm' or cap of the still</note> upon it and attach a **receiver** <note>a vessel designed to catch and condense the vapors</note>, and distill in a sand bath whatever will pass over. If everything does not rise above the **Salt** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note>, then take it out and melt it again, and pour the **liquid** <note>original: Liquor</note> back upon it and digest it again for 4 weeks according to the art, and this is done for a third time. When everything has finally come over, distill this wonderful liquid once more by itself <note>original: per se</note>, so that it may be freed from all **phlegm** <note>the watery, tasteless, and inactive liquid that comes over first during distillation</note>.

This is now the true **Alcahest of Oare** <term>Alcahest: A hypothetical universal solvent capable of reducing any body to its first matter; "Oare" likely refers to a specific proprietary name or location associated with the recipe.</term> of the famous **Fabre** <note>Pierre-Jean Fabre (1588–1658), a celebrated French physician and alchemist known for his works on the "Alkahest".</note>. Pour 10 parts of this upon 1 part of purified **Mercury** <note>alchemical symbol: ☿</note>; it opens it up. Pour this liquid again onto fresh **Mercury** and proceed exactly as described before, doing so 7 times; thus you obtain a noble **Alcahest of Oare**, with which wonders can be performed. Pour 4 parts of this onto 1 part of **Gold** <note>alchemical symbol: ☉</note> and **Antimony** <note>alchemical symbol: ♆</note> and, according to the art, in a vial with the **Seal of Hermes** <note>original: cum sigillo Herm:; meaning the glass neck is melted shut to create an airtight seal</note>, let it pass through the colors <note>The "succession of colors" (black, white, yellow, red) indicating progress in the alchemical work</note> on our **graduated furnace** <note>a furnace designed to provide precisely controlled, increasing stages of heat</note>; with the liquid, the work is augmented. [inex]pressible colors <note>original: [aus]sprechlichen; likely referring to the "unspeakable" or "inexpressible" colors that appear during the alchemical process</note> enter into fixation, since in no thing in the world is more of such a penetrating color to be found than in this, and this is the most secret work.

## How one should make the Alkahest from herbs.

It should be well noted that if one wishes to make the Alkahest <term>Alkahest: A hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any body into its primary liquid matter.</term> from herbs, one should first place them into stone vessels, press them together firmly, and, well-secured, let them putrefy <note>the process of organic decay used to release the "spirit" of the plant</note> in gentle digestion <note>long-term exposure to mild heat</note> for 3 months; then put the decayed matter into a flask and distill all the spirits over in a **water bath** <note>original: B. M. (Balneum Mariae); a vessel of boiling water used to heat a secondary container evenly</note>. Rectify <note>purify through repeated distillation</note> the spirit 7 times so that all **phlegm** <note>the watery, tasteless, and energetically "dead" part of the distillate</note> is removed from it, and it becomes sharp and penetrating. Save the phlegm; place the brown **Earth** <note>alchemical symbol: ▽; the solid residue remaining after distillation</note> that remained in the flask into a large **Retort** <note>alchemical symbol: ⚗; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck</note>, attach a receiver, and drive over the **Mercury** <note>alchemical symbol: ☿; the volatile essence or "life force" of the plant</note>, **Oil** <note>alchemical symbol: 🜔</note>, and volatile **Salt** <note>original: ominus</note> from the sand bath **by degrees** <note>original: p gradus (per gradus); slowly increasing the heat</note>; bring these three together.

Note: here at the end you must apply a strong **Fire** <note>alchemical symbol: △</note>, so that the **Retort** glows through and through, and distill it over **according to the art** <note>original: m. e. a. (modo et arte); the proper professional method</note>. However, it is better to distill from a **Retort** so that all phlegm is separated from it; add this to the above. Keep the **Oil**, Spirit, and **Mercury** well together; take the coals <note>the charred remains</note> out of the **Retort**, burn them to ashes, and with the phlegm make a **Salt** <note>original: ominus</note> from them.

Leave 1 part of the **watery residue** <note>Phlegma</note> behind; in this way, you will obtain a penetrating **solvent** <note>original: Menstruum</note> of great power, capable of extracting the **quintessence** <note>original: Q.E. or Quinta Essentia; the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</note> from all **plants** <note>original: Vegetabilien</note> and **minerals**. See to it that you separate the **solvent** from the **volatile earth** and store it well; or, if you wish, you may immediately add it to the **double liquor** and distill it once more **according to the rules of the art** <note>original: m. e. a. or modo et arte</note> so that all the watery residue is removed. It is better, however, to leave the **earth** out of it until the **liquor** is united with the **quicksilver** <note>original: ☿viv. or Mercurius vivus; liquid mercury</note>, as you can add the earth to it afterward and distill it once or twice more.

Take a well-purified **mercury** <term>☿</term>, squeeze it through leather <note>a traditional method of filtering liquid mercury to remove physical impurities</note> so that it becomes quite clean. It also does no harm to wash it several times beforehand with the **watery residue** <note>Phlegma</note> and then dry it thoroughly again. Take 4 **lots** <note>a historical unit of weight; 4 lots is approximately 2 ounces or 60 grams</note> of this, and pour 12 **lots** of our **universal solvent** <note>original: Alcahest; the fabled "all-solvent" sought by alchemists</note> upon it. Place this **according to the rules of the art** into a **vial** or **pelican** <note>a specialized circulatory vessel with "arms" that return condensed vapors to the base</note> and set it **according to the art** in gentle **putrefaction**, once everything is well **sealed** <note>original: lutirt; closed with luting or airtight sealant</note>, so...

...then you will see how one mercury will consume the other, and how these [mercuries] will fight with one another until the one has overcome the other and turned into a milk-white **water** <term>▽</term> <note>Symbol for Water; here referring to a liquid state of the matter</note>. Take again 4 **Loth** <note>a historical German unit of weight, approximately 15-18 grams or half an ounce</note> of **mercury** <term>☿ii</term> <note>Symbol for Mercury or Quicksilver</note> and pour the **liquid** <note>Liquor</note> upon it and let it **dissolve** <note>original: solviren</note>. When the **mercury** <term>☿</term> is all dissolved, separate the liquid from the **dregs** <note>Fecibus</note> again; you can repeat this with fresh **mercury** until the liquid has become like a thick milk. Thus you have the true **Virgin’s Milk** <note>original: Jungfer-Milch; a common alchemical term for a white, milky suspension or solvent often used as a precursor to the Philosopher's Stone</note>, which is then distilled once more through an **alembic** <term>⚗</term> <note>Symbol for a pear-shaped distillation vessel or retort</note> with very gentle **fire** <term>△</term> <note>Symbol for Fire or heat</note>. This is now the true **Alcahest** <note>the fabled universal solvent of the alchemists, said to be able to reduce any substance to its primary liquid form</note>, which the greatest masters of the Art have possessed.

### One also makes an Alcahest as follows.

Collect both materials from a healthy person at the same time—one who drinks wine, however—into an oak barrel; namely, **excrement** <note>original: Stercus; dung</note> and **urine** <term>⊡</term> <note>Symbol for Urine</note>. Keep them well-covered and let them ferment and **putrefy** <note>original: putreficiren; a process of slow digestion or rotting through gentle warmth to unlock the "spirit" of the matter</note> for 3 to 4 months. Then, place it by the **fire** <term>△</term> for 1 month, as was mentioned in the previous work, and following that, the work is the same in all respects.

## How to Make an Important Alcahest in the Following Way.

Take the materials when you are fresh and healthy and have drunk a good wine; then keep the surplus materials <note>The "surplus materials" collected after drinking wine refer to human urine, a common starting point in 17th-century recipes for the *Alcahest* or universal solvent</note>, and let them **putrefy** <note>original: putreficiren; the process of biological decay used to break down substances</note> for 40 **days and nights** <term>♂ ♃</term> <note>Symbols for Mars and Jupiter; here used as shorthand for a specific duration of time, likely 40 full days</note>. Then put both together into an **alembic** <term>⚗</term> <note>A glass or ceramic vessel used for distillation</note> and **distill** <note>original: destillire</note> off all moisture. 

At the beginning, use a very gentle **fire** <term>△</term>. When all the **watery phlegm** <note>original: Phlegma; the tasteless, watery part of a distillate</note> has come over, along with the foul-smelling **dregs** <note>original: Feces; the solid or sedimented waste</note>, remove the **receiver** <note>original: Vorlage; the vessel that catches the distilled liquid</note> and put another one in its place. When you see red drops appearing, which **congeal** <note>original: coaguliren; to thicken or solidify</note> immediately as they fall, and also white drops, it is time to quickly attach another receiver, **seal it** <note>original: lutire; to seal the joints of the apparatus with "lute," a clay-like paste, to prevent vapors from escaping</note> well, and drive it over completely with the **fourth degree** <note>indicated by 'p' or '4'; the highest level of heat in alchemical practice, often involving a glowing furnace</note> of heat. Finally, let the whole apparatus **glow** <note>original: erglüe; to heat until red-hot</note>, so that the receiver will be filled with white vapor. 

When no more drops fall and all the vapor has disappeared, let the **fire** <term>△</term> go out. The **salt** <term>🜔</term> <note>Symbol for Salt; specifically a volatile salt produced by the high heat</note> has congealed, and you will find in your receiver a white and red **salt** <term>🜔</term>, which will not unite. If you wish to remove the **salt** <term>🜔</term>, you must warm the receiver thoroughly so the **salt** <term>🜔</term> becomes liquid, and then pour it out; however, this must be done quickly, for it soon congeals again.

This red **salt** <term>🜔</term> now transforms every **silver** <term>☽</term> into permanent **gold** <term>☉</term>, provided the silver is first made into a **mercury** <term>☿</term> <note>A "mercury" of a metal refers to its liquid, purified essence or a metallic amalgam</note> without any **corrosive** <note>original: Corrosiv; an acid or caustic substance used to dissolve metals</note>, and this **salt** <term>🜔</term> is poured upon it.

This ensures that the **dregs** <note>original: Feces</note> always remain in the **retort** <term>🜃</term>, and the pure substances come away from them.

...[black]<note>The word fragment "ze" likely concludes "schwarze" (black) from the previous page.</note> stinking **dregs** <term>Feces</term> remain behind, pour the **liquid** <term>Liquor</term> back onto 4 **ounces** <term>Loth</term> <note>a historical German unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams or half an ounce</note> of fresh **mercury** <term>☿</term> and proceed again as before, and do this 7 times; then take your **liquid**, to which you have added the **virgin water** <term>jungfräuliche ▽</term>, and weigh it exactly. ### A powerful Alcahest is also prepared in the following way.

Then filter it again into a flask and distill half of the entire volume; place the flask in the cellar and let it stand for a time, so that all the volatile part of the **Salt** <term>$\ominus$</term> <note>alchemical symbol for salt</note> will **crystallize** <note>original: *anschiessen*, the process where crystals "shoot out" or form in a cooling liquid</note>. Collect this together, distill another portion of what remained, and put it back in the cellar, so that even more **Salt** <term>$\ominus$</term> will crystallize; take this out as well and add it to the above, and repeat the distillation for a third time, so that a little more will crystallize, which you should also collect with the rest.

Make this **Salt** <term>Sal. $\ominus$</term> <note>original: *Sal*, Latin for salt</note> very dry, grind it small, and mix it into burnt clay and **water** <term>$\nabla$</term> <note>alchemical symbol for water or moisture</note>; make balls from this, and when they are dry, fill a **Retort** <term>$\curvearrowright$</term> <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> with them. **Lute** <note>to seal the joints of the glass apparatus with a paste or clay to prevent vapors from escaping</note> a large receiver to it, and by increasing the degrees of heat over a long duration—at least 48 hours—drive out a very volatile **Spirit** and a strong **Acid**. 

When nothing more wishes to come over, let everything cool, remove the receiver, and pour your **Chaos** <note>the raw, unrefined liquid distillate containing a mixture of various substances</note> into a flask that is not too high. Place it in a **Water Bath** <note>original: *B. M.* or *Balneum Mariae*, a vessel of hot water used to heat laboratory glass gently and evenly</note> and, with everything well-sealed, distill over a volatile **Spirit** (though it is better to do this from a **Retort** <term>$\curvearrowright$</term>). **Rectify** <note>to purify a liquid by repeated distillation</note> this spirit until all the **phlegm** <note>original: *Phlegma*, the watery, tasteless, and medically "dead" part of a distillate</note> is gone from it, and pour these two together again. Distill them over once more <note>original: *m. e. a.*</note> and preserve this well. 

Then take all the **Sal Ammoniac** <term>$\ast$</term> <note>a white crystalline salt, ammonium chloride, used as a powerful reagent</note>, once the Spirit is well **dephlegmated** <note>the process of removing water from a spirit</note>, and rectify it also; bring both together and distill them over once more. Take the [residue] remaining behind in the **Retort** <term>$\curvearrowright$</term>...

This <term>salt</term> <note>original: ⊝; alchemical symbol for vitriol or salt</note> can be used for the cementation <note>a process of refining or hardening metals by heating them in contact with chemical powders</note> of <term>silver</term> <note>original: ☽; the symbol for the Moon, representing silver</note>, because it is fixed <note>stable and non-volatile</note> to the highest degree. S., standing for the Latin *stratum super stratum*</note>. This process makes the silver fixed and <term>fire-resistant</term> <note>original: △; the symbol for fire</note>, equal to gold in every way. The cementation must be performed several times, however, as this salt "robs" or consumes very little of the metal. This fixed silver can then be hammered into thin sheets and thrown into a gradating <term>fire</term> <note>original: 🜂; a heat level used to refine or "grade" the quality of metals</note>; there, they will turn into pure <term>gold</term> <note>original: ☉; the symbol for the Sun, representing gold</note> and possess a high color.

### Thus an Alcahest is also prepared, as follows:

One takes the material that settles in the barrels of young wines and sinks to the bottom. Dyers also use it, and the matter is of two kinds: the first becomes compact and is called <term>tartar</term> <note>original: ♀; while usually the symbol for Venus/Copper, the scribe uses it here for *Tartarus* or wine-stone</note>. The other kind is in liquid form and is called "yeast" <note>original: *Hefen*; the lees of wine</note> by the common man. Of this, one takes 10 to 12 cans, separated from all phlegm <note>the watery, tasteless part of a distilled substance</note> where possible. Place these into various vessels and set them to putrefaction. ## The True Great Work [*Opus maximum*].

In July and August, go to a place where many men constantly leave their <term>urine</term> <term>⚳</term> and **excrement** [*Stercora*], where the sun shines intensely. This <term>Character</term> <note>A magical sigil, seal, or symbolic mark believed to hold spiritual power.</note> has great power and strength to stir <term>visions</term> <note>original: Visiones</note> or sights in sleep. The forefather and patriarch Jacob possessed and used this on his journey when he traveled to Mesopotamia and had a stone for a pillow, under which he laid this <term>Pentacle</term> <note>A ritual object or inscribed symbol, often used for protection or to facilitate communication with the divine.</note>; in his sleep, he saw the high ladder into heaven, upon which the angels ascended and descended. However, the <term>operator</term> <note>The person performing the ritual.</note> must be chaste, pure, and God-fearing, especially if he wishes to achieve a good result. He placed this Pentacle <note>A geometric amulet or talisman used in ritual magic.</note> beneath it and saw in his sleep the high ladder reaching into heaven, upon which the angels ascended and descended. However, the practitioner <note>original: Operateur; the person performing the ritual.</note> must be chaste, pure, and god-fearing; especially if he wishes to achieve a good result, he must pray diligently, particularly the following prayer. He must not allow himself to be distracted, though there may be calling, shouting, throwing, knocking, or other obstacles <note>original: Impedimenta; spiritual disturbances or "terrors" intended to break the ritualist's concentration.</note> presented; the practitioner must simply proceed with courage and then immediately go to bed and fall asleep in the name of God, and he will obtain his desire. At the beginning, he shall fumigate the room with mastic, white frankincense, and eaglewood <note>original: Kylo Aloes; a precious, fragrant wood used in incense.</note>, then light two candles that are still whole, and place them on the two corners of the table toward the East <note>original: gegen den Morgen; literally "toward the morning."</note>. He shall turn his face toward the sunrise, kneel, and thus pray with the highest devotion:

> Holy, holy, holy, our King Jesus Christ, on Wednesday he was sold and betrayed.

### To Make an Alkahest from Bread.

An alkahest <note>a hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of reducing any body to its prime matter</note> is made in the following manner: take a proper white bread, cut it or, better yet, break it into small pieces; fill a large <term>retort</term> <note>represented by a retort symbol; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note> with them, place it in a sand-bath <note>original: Sand-Capelle; a container filled with sand used to provide even, indirect heat</note>, with a receiver well-luted <note>sealed with an airtight alchemical paste or clay</note>; when everything is dry, then apply <term>fire</term> <note>represented by a triangle symbol</note> by degrees for 16 hours, so that in the 1st degree some phlegm <note>original: Phlegma; in alchemy, this refers to the tasteless, watery part of a distillate</note> comes over...

The above Alkahest can also be united with **living mercury** <note>original: "☿ viv." (mercurius vivus)</note> and the Alkahest for metals can be prepared from it, just as I will guide your hand in the preceding and following sections.

## This is also how one makes an Alkahest:

Take **Manna**, which is a coagulated, light-filled, sweet **astral salt** <note>original symbol: ⊖; "Manna" in alchemy often refers to a celestial or atmospheric substance collected from dew</note>, 6 **pounds** <note>original symbol: ℔</note>, grind it small, and pour onto it a pure and genuine **May-dew** <note>Dew collected in the early morning during spring, believed to be highly charged with "world spirit"</note> only so much that it can dissolve in it; bring this solution into several glasses and seal them well; place them in gentle heat for 4 months to undergo **putrefaction** <note>A stage of fermentation or decay meant to unlock the internal spirit of the matter</note>, then process it as is customary and as I shall further teach you in this secret **ecstasy** <note>referring to a state of spiritual insight or a specific alchemical revelation</note>; thus you will obtain such a powerful Alkahest, with which the ancients performed wonders. From this work, you obtain a **glowing oil** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>; when this is **fixed** by itself <note>made stable so it no longer evaporates under heat</note> and placed in a glass, ignited with a small glowing coal, and the glass quickly melted shut, the **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note> is extinguished, yet it remains burning and shining like a flash of lightning. In this way, either by itself or with **gold and antimony** <note>original symbols: ☉ and ♆</note>, such a high **Quintessence** <note>original: "Q. E." (Quinta Essentia), the purest medicinal core of a substance</note> and **panacea** <note>a universal medicine</note> for the human body is prepared; only 2 to 3 **grains** <note>a tiny unit of weight</note> are to be used, but no more, so that the **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note> in nature does not consume itself with equal **fire**.

### Another Alkahest.

Note, one also makes an excellent Alkahest in the following manner. Take the <term>Quinta Essentia</term> <note>The "Fifth Essence," or the most purified, spiritual concentration of a substance</note> of the herbs and flowers which the bees collect—called Honey—and dissolve it in <term>May-dew</term> <note>Dew collected in spring, believed by alchemists to be saturated with celestial life-force</note>, using only as much as is sufficient. Place the solution into various glasses, seal them well with luting clay, and set them aside to <term>putrefy</term> <note>A process of slow fermentation or rotting intended to release the internal "spirit" of the matter</note> for 3 to 4 months. Afterward, process it as was mentioned regarding **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ⚃</note>; in this way, you will obtain a wonderful Alkahest, magnificent for the human body, and when it is united with **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, it is also effective upon the metals.

### Furthermore, a secret Alkahest is made thus:

Take the flesh or heart and brain from one who has been executed with the sword <note>Alchemical traditions often held that the "vital spirits" remained concentrated in the bodies of those who died suddenly or violently</note>, or from a fallen stag, or from a pig. Take the heart and brain, and also some of the flesh, so that it totals 12 **pounds** <note>original symbol: ℔</note> in weight. Chop it small, and for every pound, pour over it 1 can of **Urine** <note>original symbol: ⊡</note> from a man who constantly drinks wine and is in his prime age. Place it into stone vessels and let it <term>putrefy</term> for 3 months, well-sealed in the proper manner. Then, place as much of this as is sufficient into a large **Retort** <note>original symbol: ⚗; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note>, set it in a sand-bath with a large, well-luted receiver attached, and begin to distill until all the spirits...

### To make an Alcahest by the dry path.
<note>original: "Per viam siccam einen Alcahest zu machen." The Alcahest was the legendary "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, capable of dissolving any substance into its primary matter. The "dry path" refers to a method using high heat and crucibles rather than liquid-based digestion.</note>

Thus one also makes a secret Alcahest in a short time as follows: take 4 <term>pounds</term> <note>original symbol: ℔</note> of <term>tin</term> <note>original symbol: ♃ (Jupiter)</note>, grind it small, and place it into various stone vessels. Pour 20 cans of fresh <term>urine</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>—taken from a young person who drinks wine—upon it, and let it <term>putrefy</term> <note>the process of rotting or fermenting to break down a substance</note> as is customary for at least 4 months. Then, distill all the <term>volatile spirits</term> over from an <term>alembic</term> <note>original symbol: ⚗; a teardrop-shaped glass vessel used for distillation</note>. <term>Rectify</term> <note>purify by distilling multiple times</note> these spirits 7 times and store them well. Let all the <term>phlegm</term> <note>the watery, inactive part of the distillate</note> evaporate in a stone pot until the "white spirits" begin to rise. Then, when this liquid is cold, place it in a <term>flask</term> <note>original: Kolben; a rounded glass vessel</note> with a large, wide <term>alembic head</term> <note>original: Helm; the "cap" of the still</note> and <term>receiver</term> <note>original: Vorlage; the vessel that catches the condensed liquid</note>, ensuring everything is well-<term>luted</term> <note>sealed airtight with a specialized paste or clay</note>. Distill everything over from a sand bath according to the <term>degrees</term> <note>referring to the gradual stages of increasing heat</note>, and finally apply a strong <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> so that the flask glows red; in this way, you will obtain a powerful spirit and much volatile <term>salt</term> <note>original symbol: ⦵</note>. Then let the fire go out.

**Note Well:** Large, wide flasks must be used for this work because this liquid rises significantly, and the openings of the alembic head must also be wide and large; otherwise, they would be blocked by the volatile salt, and the work would be ruined. Take all the salt out of the head, put—

## Another Alcahest is made in this way:

Take 3 **pounds** <note>original symbol: ℔</note> of refined and purified **sal ammoniac** <term>$\ominus$</term> <note>ammonium chloride</note>. Dissolve it in its own mother liquor—which remains behind as a thick grease—using 6 pitchers of the same. Place the vessels in gentle warmth for 4 months to **putrefy** <note>the process of breaking down or "rotting" a substance to release its internal components</note>. Then pour your liquid into a **retort** <term>⚗</term> <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note> and distill all the **phlegm** <note>the watery, inactive portion of a distillate</note> over. It is better to collect the phlegm in a flask; save it all separately. When the **spirits** <note>the volatile, active essence of the substance</note> begin to rise, quickly attach another receiver and continue distilling until all the volatile spirit has come over. Then let the **fire** <term>$\triangle$</term> go out. **Rectify** <note>purify a liquid by repeated distillation</note> the spirit as many times as needed until all the phlegm is removed from it. Take out what remained in the flask, grind it small, and mix into it a burnt and leached **clay** <note>original: Letten; a type of loam or fatty clay used in alchemical processes</note>. Form this into **globes** <note>small balls or pellets</note>, and when they are dry, fill a retort with them and distill over an open **flame** <term>$\triangle$</term> by **degrees** <note>original: per gradus; gradually increasing the temperature</note> until nothing more passes over. **Rectify** the distillate through a retort as many times as necessary until all phlegm is gone. Pour this into the spirit mentioned above and distill it all over once more. Then, keep it well secured. Take the globes out of the retort, grind them small, and extract them with the saved phlegm...

### The first work of the Ancients proceeds from the Hyleal <note>relating to 'Hyle,' the fundamental primordial matter of the universe</note>, that is, the prime-material or universal <note>original: "Catholischen"; here meaning "all-encompassing" rather than religious</note> general Chaos.

Take a red <term>Earth</term> <note>original symbol: 🜃</note>, loam, or clay. The clay-like loam is the best among these three. Take from such earth, dug out at least 3 cubits <note>original: "Ellen"; an old unit of length approximately 18 to 22 inches</note> deep, so that it has not already been determined toward something else by the stars, Sun, and Moon; bring it into a barrel and protect it well, so that the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> <note>likely referring to the heat of the sun or external elements</note> does not impart anything astral to it. As soon as the dew falls, collect as much dew as you can, even if you could obtain 100 measures. It is good to catch it in May, June, and July; store it in stone jars in a cool place, so that it does not alter <note>spoil or lose its energetic potency</note>.

When you have thus prepared your <term>Salt of Metals</term> <note>original: ⊖ Metallorisch</note>, keep it as a great treasure. Take a well-purified <term>gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and dissolve it in a good <term>Aqua Regis</term> <note>original: R.; a highly corrosive mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold</note>. Precipitate it with an <term>Oil of Vitriol</term> <note>original: ⸮ ♀ri; concentrated sulfuric acid</note>, wash it thoroughly until it is "sweet" <note>to "sweeten" in alchemy means to wash away all traces of corrosive acids or salts with water</note>, and dry it gently. However, before you dry it completely, mix a third part of <term>Flowers of Antimony</term> <note>original: Flores ♁; a white vaporous oxide formed by heating antimony</note> into it and let the moisture evaporate. By doing this, you need not worry that the mixture will detonate <note>gold precipitates, known as "fulminating gold," are notoriously unstable and can explode if dried without stabilizing agents like antimony</note>. At the last, burn away the <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> and wash the <term>mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; here referring to the metallic calx or residue</note> well with distilled water; thus it is prepared for calcination.

Alternatively, have the <term>gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> beaten into leaves and amalgamate it with pure <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: ☿ vivo; liquid mercury</note> until the <term>mercury</term> has swallowed all the <term>gold</term>. Place the metal in a <term>retort</term> <note>original symbol: ⚗; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note>, set it in a sand bath, and seal a receiver to it with <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note>. Drive over your <term>mercury</term> through heat; in the <term>retort</term>, your <term>gold</term> will remain, free of all corrosive substances. This <term>gold-mercury</term> <note>original: ☉ ☿; likely the purified gold sponge or calx left after mercury is distilled away</note> must then be reverberated <note>heated in a furnace where the flames are reflected or "reverberated" down onto the material</note> in a glass furnace, as previously mentioned, until it swells up like a rose; then it is prepared.

Now, if you wish to prepare for yourself a precious carbuncle or ruby, take 2 <term>Loth</term> <note>A unit of weight roughly equal to 15 grams or half an ounce.</note> of this <term>gold-mercury</term> and 1 <term>Quintlein</term> <note>original: Quentl.; a small unit of weight, about 3.75 grams</note> of the <term>Salt of Metals</term> <note>original: ⊖ Metallor.</note>. Grind them together and let them sit in a good, strong, glazed crucible <note>original symbol: ♒; the source notes this symbol indicates the state of fire or melting in this context</note> within a glass furnace. Cover it with another crucible. When it has become entirely <term>molten</term> <note>original symbol: ♒</note>, lift the crucible while still hot out of the furnace, keeping it covered, and place it in the cooling oven so that it may cool down. Beware during the <term>melting</term> <note>original symbol: ♒</note> that nothing falls into the vessel. Once it has flowed for two hours, it is finished.

### The Sixth Secret Operation from the Ore of Antimony <note>original: "Minera Antimonii"</note>

Take a proper **antimony ore** <note>original: "min. ♁ii"</note> that has been separated from all rocky matter, and crush it so small it becomes an almost imperceptible **powder** <note>symbol: ♁</note>. Take 3 **pounds** <note>symbol: ℔</note> of this, and **calcine** <note>roast at high heat to drive off impurities</note> it on a roasting dish until it becomes a gray ash. Place this in a good **crucible** <note>a ceramic container used for melting materials at high temperatures</note> and set it in a furnace; leave it for about 2 hours, then pour it into a warmed mortar. You will thus obtain a red **glass** <note>original: "Vitrum"; a translucent, glassy form of antimony</note>. Crush and grind this small again and put it into a **flask** <note>original: "Kolben"</note>. Pour a distilled **vinegar** <note>symbol: 🜔; note: the previous page identifies this symbol as vitriol, but in this process it refers to distilled vinegar used to extract the color from the glass</note> over it. Secure the flask with a **blind head** <note>a distillation cap without a spout, used to keep vapors inside for "digestion"</note> and place it in gentle heat. Let it stand for 4 weeks, and your **vinegar** <note>symbol: 🜔</note> will be colored like blood. Pour this off and add fresh liquid, continuing the extraction until no more redness can be drawn out. Then combine all the colored **vinegar** and **distill** it from a **retort** <note>symbol: 🝪; a glass vessel with a long, curving neck</note> until only a thick red juice remains. Onto this juice, pour distilled **water** <note>symbol: ▽</note> and extract it again. Pour off the clear **solution**, as some **dregs** <note>original: "Feces"; the solid waste or residue</note> will settle again. Distill the **water** <note>symbol: ▽</note> from a flask until it becomes a red juice once more. Extract this again with distilled **water**, and distill it back down to a red juice. Onto this, pour highly rectified **spirit of wine** <note>symbol: 🜵; high-proof alcohol</note> and extract it one more time. Pour off the clear **solution** so that all **dregs** are removed. Combine the clear **solutions**

By roasting the ore into "glass" and using various solvents like vinegar, water, and alcohol, the alchemist isolates the concentrated essence of the metal.</summary>

...distill all the **spirits of wine** <term>original: "Spiritus Vini"; high-proof alcohol used as a solvent</term> off from it. If you wished, you could distill your entire **remedy** <note>original: "R.", likely standing for "Res" (matter) or "Remedium" (remedy)</note> over by **cohobation** <term>the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid over its own solid residue to concentrate its "virtue"</term>, but it is not necessary. Set it upon our **graduating furnace** <note>original: "Gradofen"; a furnace designed to provide specific, incremental levels of heat</note> to **fix** <term>to transform a volatile or liquid substance into a solid that can withstand heat without evaporating</term> it. In this way, you will obtain a great **matter** <note>original: "R."</note> acting upon **live mercury** <note>original: "☿ vivum"; liquid quicksilver</note> and a mighty **panacea** <term>a universal medicine believed to cure all ailments and prolong life</term>. Take 1 to 2 **grains** <note>a tiny unit of weight, about the size of a cereal grain</note> for the **preservation** and **renewal** of human life.

<meta>This page serves as a title page for a new section of the manuscript. While the previous pages detailed the "Sixth Secret Operation" of an alchemical process—focusing on the creation of a universal medicine or panacea—this page introduces a series of letters concerning the Freemasons in Vienna. In the late 18th century, alchemy, Rosicrucianism, and Freemasonry were often deeply interconnected in Central European intellectual and esoteric circles. The "bleed-through" noted in the transcription suggests this title page was written on the reverse of the preceding alchemical instructions or vice-versa.</meta>

# L E T T E R S

### OF AN HONEST MAN <note>original: "Biedermann"; a term describing an upright, plain-dealing, and moral citizen. It was a popular pseudonym in the 18th century for authors wishing to present themselves as unbiased and virtuous observers of society.</note>

->TO<-

### AN HONEST MAN

->CONCERNING THE<-

# FREEMASONS

### IN VIENNA.

---

->Munich, 1786.<-

<summary>This is the title page for a collection of letters regarding the Freemasonry movement in Vienna, published in 1786. It signals a shift from technical alchemical instructions to a sociological or polemical discussion of fraternal organizations during the Enlightenment.</summary>
